Well, I wouldn't say it's completely upending my life or anything. I try my best to keep on top of things, I really do. But, um... I suppose it is getting in the way a little bit more than I'd like to admit.

Or I'll go to do a little repair job and realize I showed up without the right tools. \n\nIt's a bit embarrassing to admit, really, because I always try to be reliable. But my family thought I should come talk to you about it. Does that make sense? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Are these memory lapses interfering with your daily routine or work?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say it's completely upending my life or anything. I try my best to keep on top of things, I really do. But, um... I suppose it is getting in the way a little bit more than I'd like to admit. \n\nLike I mentioned with my heart doctor... it's actually gotten to the point where their office has had to call and chase me down about those missed follow-ups. I always feel just awful about it when they do, I really hate wasting their time. And, well, I help out with a repair group down at the senior center. Just small jobs, you know? But lately... it's gotten to where they just can't count on me the way they used to. \n\nI really pride myself on being helpful, so letting them down is... well, it's a bit hard to talk about, honestly. I hope I'm not complaining too much. Does that answer your question okay? Visual instructions? Like maps and such? Oh, no, I don't believe so. I'm actually quite alright with those.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Forgetting things I planned to do? Well... I suppose I do have a little bit of trouble with that sometimes. Nothing too terrible, I don't think, but... well, actually, it's sort of what brings me in to see you today. 

I've been... well, I keep forgetting things that I really shouldn't. Like, my heart follow-up appointments. I've missed a couple, and I feel just awful about it because the nurses are so sweet and I hate to waste their time. And sometimes I forget to call the clinic back when they leave a message. Or, you know, down at the center, I'll promise to fix something and then show up without the right tools for the repair job. I just feel so foolish when that happens, letting the other fellas down like that. 

I try my best to be dependable, I really do. It's just... sometimes things slip away from me before I get around to doing them. I hope I'm not sounding too irresponsible, Doctor? I really am trying to keep on top of things. Does that make sense?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Written down? Oh... well, no, not usually. If I write it down, I'm usually pretty good about it. 

I mean, I try to keep a little notepad by the phone for that exact reason. If I make a note of it, or if one of the nurses writes an appointment card for me, I'll stick it right on the refrigerator. Once it's up there, I generally don't miss it. It's really just those times when I think, "Oh, I'll remember to do that later," and I don't write it down... that's when I get into a bit of trouble. 

But if I actually take the time to make a reminder, I do follow through. I'd hate for you to think I'm completely disorganized! I do try very hard to be responsible when I have a system in place. Is that the kind of thing you're asking about, Doctor? I hope I'm answering these right for you.

Well, if I haven't written it down... yes, I suppose that's exactly when things tend to slip my mind.

Well, yes... I suppose that's exactly it. A mental note just doesn't seem to stick the way it used to.

I'll go in meaning to pay real close attention to everyone. But after a little while, my mind just sort of... slides off. \n\nBefore I know it, I realize I missed half of what was said. I always try to be as clear as possible so I don't hold up the conversation or confuse anybody. \n\nIs that... does that answer your question okay? I really hope I'm giving you what you need here. I'm trying to be as thorough as I can for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, changes in my mood? No, I don't think so. I'm usually a pretty upbeat fella! I always try to keep a smile on my face, you know? I wouldn't want to bring anyone down at the senior center by being grumpy or complaining. \n\nSo, no, no big swings or feeling down or anything like that. I just try to stay busy and keep positive. I've always thought it's better to just keep moving and not dwell on things, so my mood has been just fine. \n\nI mean, I hope that's alright? I'm trying my best to give you good answers here. Does that help with what you're looking for, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention to what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose... maybe just a little bit sometimes. I mean, I always try my absolute best to stay focused, I really do. But, well, like if I sit down at the bridge table, or when we have our Fix-It Friends planning meetings at the center... I'll go in meaning to pay real close attention to everyone. But after a little while, my mind just sort of... slides off. \n\nBefore I know it, I realize I missed half of what was said. It's a little embarrassing, to be honest with you. I always feel terrible because I don't want the other guys to think I'm ignoring them or that I don't care, because I really do! I just have a hard time keeping my brain pinned down on one thing for too long. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble making decisions for yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, making decisions? No, I don't believe I have any trouble there. I'm usually pretty quick to make up my mind, actually. Whether it's deciding which broken radio to tinker with first down at the center, or just figuring out what to have for lunch... I just pick something and get right to it. \n\nI really don't like to dilly-dally, you know? I'd hate to hold anyone up or be a bother to folks by being indecisive. So I just make a choice and keep things moving along. \n\nSo, no, that's always been pretty smooth sailing for me. I hope that's a good thing? I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right details. Am I doing okay with these questions, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble planning or organizing tasks?\n\nPatient: Planning or organizing? Oh, no, I don't believe so. I've always been pretty good about keeping my ducks in a row. \n\nYou know, like with my tools down at the center—I always make sure I have everything laid out neat and tidy before I start tinkering with a broken fan or a toaster or what have you. I'd hate to leave a big mess lying around for one of the other fellas to trip over, that just wouldn't be very polite. So I try to be real dependable about keeping my projects organized. I just get everything set up and go right to town on it. \n\nSo... no, no problems there, I'd say. I hope that's a helpful answer? I'm really trying my best to give you exactly what you need to know, Doctor. Am I doing alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble understanding or following visual instructions, like diagrams or maps?\n\nPatient: Visual instructions? Like maps and such? Oh, no, I don't believe so. I'm actually quite alright with those. \n\nWhen I'm working on a broken toaster or a radio down at the center, I often have to look at the little diagrams or schematics in the manuals. I can follow those pictures just fine. I'd feel awful if I broke someone's appliance just because I couldn't read the instructions right, you know? So I always make sure I'm doing exactly what the picture shows. Same goes for road maps, if I ever need to use one. \n\nSo, I think I'm okay in that department. I hope that's a good thing? Just let me know if I'm not giving you enough detail, Doctor. I really want to be as helpful as possible for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened recently, like what you did yesterday or last week?\n\nPatient: Remembering things? Like yesterday or last week? Oh, no, my memory is still sharp as a tack, I'd say. \n\nI don't have any trouble recalling what I had for breakfast or who I chatted with down at the senior center. I'd feel just terrible if I forgot a conversation I had with one of the other fellas or missed an appointment because it slipped my mind. I try to be very reliable about that sort of thing. People count on you, you know? So I make sure I keep track of what's going on day to day. \n\nSo, no, my memory hasn't been giving me any grief at all. I hope that's what you were looking for? I'm trying to be as clear as I can for you, Doctor. Is this all making sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget to do things you had planned to do at a later time?\n\nPatient: Forgetting things I planned to do? Well... I suppose I do have a little bit of trouble with that sometimes. Nothing too terrible, I don't think, but... well, actually, it's sort of what brings me in to see you today. \n\nI've been... well, I keep forgetting things that I really shouldn't. Like, my heart follow-up appointments. I've missed a couple, and I feel just awful about it because the nurses are so sweet and I hate to waste their time. And sometimes I forget to call the clinic back when they leave a message. Or, you know, down at the center, I'll promise to fix something and then show up without the right tools for the repair job. I just feel so foolish when that happens, letting the other fellas down like that. \n\nI try my best to be dependable, I really do. It's just... sometimes things slip away from me before I get around to doing them. I hope I'm not sounding too irresponsible, Doctor? I really am trying to keep on top of things. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself forgetting to do things that you had planned to do, even when you had previously written them down or set a reminder?\n\nPatient: Written down? Oh... well, no, not usually. If I write it down, I'm usually pretty good about it. \n\nI mean, I try to keep a little notepad by the phone for that exact reason. If I make a note of it, or if one of the nurses writes an appointment card for me, I'll stick it right on the refrigerator. Once it's up there, I generally don't miss it. It's really just those times when I think, \"Oh, I'll remember to do that later,\" and I don't write it down... that's when I get into a bit of trouble. \n\nBut if I actually take the time to make a reminder, I do follow through. I'd hate for you to think I'm completely disorganized! I do try very hard to be responsible when I have a system in place. Is that the kind of thing you're asking about, Doctor? I hope I'm answering these right for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself forgetting to do things that you had planned to do, even when you had previously intended to do them, but hadn't written them down or set a reminder?\n\nPatient: Well, if I haven't written it down... yes, I suppose that's exactly when things tend to slip my mind. \n\nLike I was saying, if I just tell myself, \"I'll do that this afternoon,\" without putting pen to paper... well, sometimes the afternoon comes and goes, and I've completely forgotten. It's not that I don't intend to do it, I really do! I just get busy tinkering with something else or chatting with the fellas, and it just floats right out of my head. \n\nI feel dreadful when it happens, especially if I promised someone I'd do something. I really don't want to seem unreliable. It's just a little hiccup with keeping things straight when I don't have my little notes to help me. I hope that's not too unusual for someone my age? I'm really trying my best to keep on top of it all, Doctor. I hope that answers your question?\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget to perform tasks that you had intended to do, even if you had previously made a mental note to do them?\n\nPatient: Well, yes... I suppose that's exactly it. A mental note just doesn't seem to stick the way it used to. \n\nI know I've mentioned it already, but... it really is the main reason I'm sitting here today. I just feel so terrible about it. I'll make a mental note to go to my heart follow-up, or to call the clinic back when they leave a message, and then... poof. It's gone. I care very much! I just... I don't know why these mental notes keep slipping away from me. It makes me feel so silly, like I'm not paying attention, but I promise I am. I hope you don't think too poorly of me for it, Doctor. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh... yes, I think you've hit the nail on the head. If it's just something I need to do eventually, without a strict clock on it, I usually manage to get to it sooner or later. It's a bit more forgiving, I suppose. 

I am so sorry, Doctor, I know I'm giving you the exact same examples again, and I really hope I'm not frustrating you. It's just... well, it really is the whole reason I'm here talking to you today. I just keep forgetting the things I absolutely shouldn't. When there's a set schedule—like those heart follow-ups, or having to call the clinic back, or showing up with the right tools for a job at a specific hour—that's when it all just falls apart on me. 

I really do try my best to be reliable, I promise I do. Am I doing okay explaining the difference for you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to know.

It's not that I don't intend to do it, I really do! I hope you don't think too poorly of me for it, Doctor. I really want to be as helpful as possible for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened recently, like what you did yesterday or last week?\n\nPatient: Remembering things? Like yesterday or last week? Oh, no, my memory is still sharp as a tack, I'd say. \n\nI don't have any trouble recalling what I had for breakfast or who I chatted with down at the senior center. I'd feel just terrible if I forgot a conversation I had with one of the other fellas or missed an appointment because it slipped my mind. I try to be very reliable about that sort of thing. People count on you, you know? So I make sure I keep track of what's going on day to day. \n\nSo, no, my memory hasn't been giving me any grief at all. I hope that's what you were looking for? I'm trying to be as clear as I can for you, Doctor. Is this all making sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget to do things you had planned to do at a later time?\n\nPatient: Forgetting things I planned to do? Well... Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself forgetting to do things that you had planned to do, even when you had previously written them down or set a reminder?\n\nPatient: Written down? It's really just those times when I think, \"Oh, I'll remember to do that later,\" and I don't write it down... that's when I get into a bit of trouble. \n\nBut if I actually take the time to make a reminder, I do follow through. I'd hate for you to think I'm completely disorganized! I do try very hard to be responsible when I have a system in place. Is that the kind of thing you're asking about, Doctor? I wouldn't want to bring anyone down at the senior center by being grumpy or complaining. \n\nSo, no, no big swings or feeling down or anything like that. I just try to stay busy and keep positive. I've always thought it's better to just keep moving and not dwell on things, so my mood has been just fine. \n\nI mean, I hope that's alright? I'm trying my best to give you good answers here. Does that help with what you're looking for, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention to what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose... maybe just a little bit sometimes. I mean, I always try my absolute best to stay focused, I really do. But, well, like if I sit down at the bridge table, or when we have our Fix-It Friends planning meetings at the center... I'll go in meaning to pay real close attention to everyone. But after a little while, my mind just sort of... slides off. \n\nBefore I know it, I realize I missed half of what was said. It's a little embarrassing, to be honest with you. I always feel terrible because I don't want the other guys to think I'm ignoring them or that I don't care, because I really do! I just have a hard time keeping my brain pinned down on one thing for too long. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble making decisions for yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, making decisions? No, I don't believe I have any trouble there. I'm usually pretty quick to make up my mind, actually. Whether it's deciding which broken radio to tinker with first down at the center, or just figuring out what to have for lunch... I just pick something and get right to it. \n\nI really don't like to dilly-dally, you know? I'd hate to hold anyone up or be a bother to folks by being indecisive. So I just make a choice and keep things moving along. \n\nSo, no, that's always been pretty smooth sailing for me. I hope that's a good thing? I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right details. Am I doing okay with these questions, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble planning or organizing tasks?\n\nPatient: Planning or organizing? Oh, no, I don't believe so. I've always been pretty good about keeping my ducks in a row. \n\nYou know, like with my tools down at the center—I always make sure I have everything laid out neat and tidy before I start tinkering with a broken fan or a toaster or what have you. I'd hate to leave a big mess lying around for one of the other fellas to trip over, that just wouldn't be very polite. So I try to be real dependable about keeping my projects organized. I just get everything set up and go right to town on it. \n\nSo... no, no problems there, I'd say. I hope that's a helpful answer? I'm really trying my best to give you exactly what you need to know, Doctor. Am I doing alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble understanding or following visual instructions, like diagrams or maps?\n\nPatient: Visual instructions? Like maps and such? Oh, no, I don't believe so. I'm actually quite alright with those. \n\nWhen I'm working on a broken toaster or a radio down at the center, I often have to look at the little diagrams or schematics in the manuals. I can follow those pictures just fine. I'd feel awful if I broke someone's appliance just because I couldn't read the instructions right, you know? So I always make sure I'm doing exactly what the picture shows. Same goes for road maps, if I ever need to use one. \n\nSo, I think I'm okay in that department. I hope that's a good thing? Just let me know if I'm not giving you enough detail, Doctor. I really want to be as helpful as possible for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened recently, like what you did yesterday or last week?\n\nPatient: Remembering things? Like yesterday or last week? Oh, no, my memory is still sharp as a tack, I'd say. \n\nI don't have any trouble recalling what I had for breakfast or who I chatted with down at the senior center. I'd feel just terrible if I forgot a conversation I had with one of the other fellas or missed an appointment because it slipped my mind. I try to be very reliable about that sort of thing. People count on you, you know? So I make sure I keep track of what's going on day to day. \n\nSo, no, my memory hasn't been giving me any grief at all. I hope that's what you were looking for? I'm trying to be as clear as I can for you, Doctor. Is this all making sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget to do things you had planned to do at a later time?\n\nPatient: Forgetting things I planned to do? Well... I suppose I do have a little bit of trouble with that sometimes. Nothing too terrible, I don't think, but... well, actually, it's sort of what brings me in to see you today. \n\nI've been... well, I keep forgetting things that I really shouldn't. Like, my heart follow-up appointments. I've missed a couple, and I feel just awful about it because the nurses are so sweet and I hate to waste their time. And sometimes I forget to call the clinic back when they leave a message. Or, you know, down at the center, I'll promise to fix something and then show up without the right tools for the repair job. I just feel so foolish when that happens, letting the other fellas down like that. \n\nI try my best to be dependable, I really do. It's just... sometimes things slip away from me before I get around to doing them. I hope I'm not sounding too irresponsible, Doctor? I really am trying to keep on top of things. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself forgetting to do things that you had planned to do, even when you had previously written them down or set a reminder?\n\nPatient: Written down? Oh... well, no, not usually. If I write it down, I'm usually pretty good about it. \n\nI mean, I try to keep a little notepad by the phone for that exact reason. If I make a note of it, or if one of the nurses writes an appointment card for me, I'll stick it right on the refrigerator. Once it's up there, I generally don't miss it. It's really just those times when I think, \"Oh, I'll remember to do that later,\" and I don't write it down... that's when I get into a bit of trouble. \n\nBut if I actually take the time to make a reminder, I do follow through. I'd hate for you to think I'm completely disorganized! I do try very hard to be responsible when I have a system in place. Is that the kind of thing you're asking about, Doctor? I hope I'm answering these right for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself forgetting to do things that you had planned to do, even when you had previously intended to do them, but hadn't written them down or set a reminder?\n\nPatient: Well, if I haven't written it down... yes, I suppose that's exactly when things tend to slip my mind. \n\nLike I was saying, if I just tell myself, \"I'll do that this afternoon,\" without putting pen to paper... well, sometimes the afternoon comes and goes, and I've completely forgotten. It's not that I don't intend to do it, I really do! I just get busy tinkering with something else or chatting with the fellas, and it just floats right out of my head. \n\nI feel dreadful when it happens, especially if I promised someone I'd do something. I really don't want to seem unreliable. It's just a little hiccup with keeping things straight when I don't have my little notes to help me. I hope that's not too unusual for someone my age? I'm really trying my best to keep on top of it all, Doctor. I hope that answers your question?\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget to perform tasks that you had intended to do, even if you had previously made a mental note to do them?\n\nPatient: Well, yes... I suppose that's exactly it. A mental note just doesn't seem to stick the way it used to. \n\nI know I've mentioned it already, but... it really is the main reason I'm sitting here today. I just feel so terrible about it. I'll make a mental note to go to my heart follow-up, or to call the clinic back when they leave a message, and then... poof. It's gone. Or I'll tell myself exactly which wrench I need for a job down at the center, and then I show up empty-handed. \n\nI really don't want you to think I don't care about my health or my friends. I care very much! I just... I don't know why these mental notes keep slipping away from me. It makes me feel so silly, like I'm not paying attention, but I promise I am. I hope you don't think too poorly of me for it, Doctor. Does that make sense, what I'm trying to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to remember to do things in the future, like upcoming appointments, rather than remembering things that have already happened?\n\nPatient: Oh, absolutely. Yes... I'd say that's exactly it. Things that already happened? I remember those clear as a bell. I can tell you all sorts of things from the past, no problem at all! I really don't want you to think my mind is completely going, I promise my memory for things that have already happened is still quite sharp. \n\nIt's really just... well, exactly like I was saying a moment ago. It's the things coming up in the future that trip me up. Honestly, that's the main thing that brought me in to see you today. I just keep forgetting future things that I really, really shouldn't. Like I mentioned, those upcoming heart follow-up appointments, or remembering to call the clinic back tomorrow, or planning to bring the right tools for a repair job later on. It's always the future plans that just seem to vanish on me. \n\nI hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor. I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right and giving you what you need. Is that the kind of difference you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to remember to do things that you need to do at a specific time in the future?\n\nPatient: Well, yes... I suppose that's exactly what I've been trying to say. If there's a specific time attached to it, like... oh, like those heart follow-up appointments I mentioned, or needing to call the clinic back before they close, it just seems to slip away from me. \n\nI really don't want to sound like a broken record, Doctor, I know I keep bringing up the same examples. It's just... well, that's really what brought me in. Forgetting to show up with the right tools for a repair job when I said I'd be there at two o'clock... it just makes me feel dreadful. I promise I'm not doing it on purpose, and I really do try to keep track of the time. I just hope you don't think I'm completely irresponsible. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to remember to do things that you need to do at a specific time in the future, rather than things that you need to do, but don't have a specific time?\n\nPatient: Oh... yes, I think you've hit the nail on the head. If it's just something I need to do eventually, without a strict clock on it, I usually manage to get to it sooner or later. It's a bit more forgiving, I suppose. \n\nI am so sorry, Doctor, I know I'm giving you the exact same examples again, and I really hope I'm not frustrating you. Am I doing okay explaining the difference for you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to remember to do things that you had planned to do in advance, like preparing for an upcoming event?\n\nPatient: Yes, exactly.
